期目標於 2029 至 2030 年將 AI 基礎設施營收占比拉高至 40%
至 50%；技術面上，次世代 1C 製程預計於 2027 年第一季進行
客戶驗證 。 綜合以上 ， 研究部預估南亞科2027 年 EPS 為 130.72
元。考量目前公司記憶體供給吃緊、需求擴大，有長期合約支持未
來成長 ，且公司具備獨特製程升級與客製化產品線成長潛力 ， 研究
部給予 5.5 倍本益比評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

⚫ 公司基本資料 南亞科(2408.TT)公司簡介 
南亞科技(2408.TT)成立於 1995 年，隸屬於台塑集團，為全球領先的動
態隨機存取記憶體(DRAM)製造大廠 。 公司生產基地主要位於台灣 ， 擁有
多樣化的產品組合，涵蓋 DDR5、LPDDR5/5X、DDR4、LPDDR4/4X、
DDR3 及 LPDDR3 等產品線 ， 產品種類達數十顆 ， 服務全球數百家客戶 。
面對傳統記憶體產業之劇烈循環波動，公司近年積極推動技術升級與結
構性轉型，除中長期積極投入 1B(16 奈米)與 1C(12 奈米)先進製程轉換
及新五廠建置，並深入布局企業級 SSD(eSSD)、SmartNIC、BMC 等
AI 伺服器基礎設施，同時與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)利
基產品，正逐步由傳統大宗商品化DRAM 廠，轉型為具備高附加價值與
高敏捷度的全方位利基型記憶體解決方案供應商 。

大幅上漲且成本結構維持穩定的推升下，單季毛利率提升至
79.49%(Q+11.60ppt / Y+100.05ppt) 。雖然員工分紅與銷售研發使營業

長鑫存儲(CXMT)技術進展與產業影響 
長鑫存儲(CXMT)於 2026 年 9 月宣佈第五代 DRAM 制程(G5)進入量產階
段。儘管目前 G5 產品仍處於客戶驗證與良率提升階段，距離大規模商
業化出貨尚需時間 ， 但已顯示中國DRAM 技術發展速度明顯超出市場原
先預期。 長鑫 HBM3E 良率已達 40%左右。市場普遍認為，長鑫在無法
取得 EUV 設備的情況下，主要透過 ArF DUV 浸潤式曝光搭配多重曝光
(Multiple Patterning) 技術推進制程微縮，因此雖然能夠達到接近國際大
廠先進 DRAM 節點的設計能力 ， 但製造成本 、 良率與生產效率仍與三星 、
SK 海力士及美光存在差距。未來能否持續提升至國際大廠 70%以上的
量產水準，將是觀察長鑫HBM 競爭力的重要指標。短期而言，長鑫技術
進步對台廠商影響有限，長鑫目前仍面臨良率提升與客戶驗證所需時
間，短期內難以快速增加有效供給，因此全球 DRAM 供應緊張的局面仍
將延續至 2027 至 2028 年。相較之下，三星、SK 海力士與美光持續將
產能轉向 HBM 產品，反而造成 DDR4 供給逐漸退出市場，進一步支撐
DDR4 價格維持強勢，有利於以 DDR4 產品為主的南亞科營運表現；然
而，中長期則需注意，長鑫 DRAM 投片量將於 2027 年底達到約 30K 
WPM，並持續擴產，若未來 G5 良率持續改善、HBM 技術逐步成熟，
加上中國市場本土化政策支持，長鑫未來在中低階 DDR5、PC DRAM
及中國本土市場的競爭力可能快速提升 ， 進而對全球DRAM 供需格局產
生實質影響。

長鑫存儲(CXMT)4F²技術 
長鑫存儲在 10 月 12 日有表示年底推出 4F²架構 DDR5 伺服器記憶體，
4F²是將每個記憶體儲存單元配置在面積相當於 4 倍 F 平方的區域內 ， 其
中 F 代表半導體制程的最小線寬，這種設計能讓同一顆晶片容納更多儲
存單元，進而提高記憶體容量。目前，主流商用 DRAM 仍採用 6F 平方
儲存單元結構。長鑫存儲表示，其4F 平方設計的核心，在於自主研發的
垂直環繞閘極(Vertical Surrounding Gate，VSG)電晶體，這項技術透過
垂直閘極架構改善漏電問題，同時大幅縮小單元面積，提高儲存密度。
SK 海力士(SK Hynix) 已於 2025 年公佈 4F 平方技術發展藍圖，另外，
根據南韓媒體今年 4 月的報導，三星電子(Samsung Electronics)計畫於
2028 年前，量產採用這項新設計、制程低於 10 奈米的 DRAM。

結論與建議 
展望未來 ， 南亞科(2408.TT) 因受限於極低庫存 ，2027 年上半年位元出
貨量預期將保持平穩 ， 待4Q27 5A 新廠開出產能後 ， 出貨量將恢復逐步
成長；產能方面，5A 新廠規劃於 2027 年第一季開始進場裝機，第一

### Page 4

4
階段預計 2028 年底前投片量達 3 萬片 ， 全產能5 萬片目標於 2030 年
底達成，且擴建嚴格以客戶 LTA 的 AI 專案為導向；此外，公司規劃於
屏東科學園區建置  3D 晶圓封裝與測試廠，預計 2028 下半年導入設
備，並與客戶合作開發客製化 AI/WoW(晶圓堆疊)與 ASIC 專案，已開
始產生部分營收貢獻，長期目標於 2029 至 2030 年將 AI 基礎設施營
收占比拉高至 40%至 50%； 技術面上 ， 次世代 1C 製程預計於 2027 年
第一季進行客戶驗證。綜合以上，研究部預估南亞科(2408.TT) 2027 年
營業收入為 7,103.14 億元，Y+69.49%、毛利率 84.00%，Y+1.39ppt、
營業利益率 78.64%，Y+1.81ppt、 稅後純益 4,510.28 億元 ，Y+70.70%、

EPS 為 130.72 元。考量目前公司記憶體供給吃緊、需求擴大，有長期
合約支持未來成長，且公司具備獨特製程升級與客製化產品線成長潛
力，依據美光27 年本益比區間約 5 至 6.6 倍，研究部給予5.5 倍本益比
評價，目標價 710 元，投資建議為買進。

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R  Powered by the EFA Platform 1

動態更新 IC製造 ‧ 台灣

台積電 
(2330.TW /2330 TT)

多、更多、再更多的 N3 產能擴張 
重要訊息 
台積電 3Q26 財報表現符合預期，管理層預估 2026 年營收將年增 44%。 
評論及分析  
3Q26 符合預期，4Q26 展望強勁。3Q26 毛利率 66.4%，受 N2 量產爬坡稀
釋影響，較我們預估低 1.6 個百分點，但符合公司財測。受惠營收成長及營
業費用控管，營業利益及營益率均符合預期。台積電 4Q26 營收財測為 523
億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增 14%，較我們先前預估高出 7%，毛
利率及營益率則預期持平，主要受惠 HPC 需求強勁及 N2 量產爬坡。 
N3 持續擴產，並評估美國亞利桑那州以外的擴產機會 。隨 4Q26 展望轉
強，台積電將 2026 年美元營收成長預估由原先 40%以上上調至 44%，並
維持全年資本支出 600 億至 640 億美元。台積電過去鮮少將單一製程產能
擴充至超過原先規劃的目標( 約每月 15 萬片)。然而，除近期宣布的三座晶圓
廠(台南 Fab 18 、亞利桑那州二廠及熊本二廠) 外，台積電再宣布於台南新增
一座 N3 晶圓廠，預計於 1H28 量產。整體而言，我們預估 N3 產能將由
2026 年底的每月 15 萬至 16 萬片，在未來兩年進一步增加 8 萬片，增幅達
50%。我們認為此次擴產反映三項需求：(1)Agentic AI 帶動的 CPU 需求；
(2)HBM4e 客製化基底晶粒；以及(3)AI ASIC 。此外，台積電亦正評估在美國
亞利桑那州以外地區擴產的可能性，例如德州，但目前尚未做出最終決定。  
不會將任何商機拱手讓給競爭對手 。管理層多次強調不會讓競爭對手取得任
何商機，體現在 N3 產能的積極擴充。此外，儘管我們預期 Google(美 )委託
Intel(美 )生產的 Humufish 目前沒有備援計劃。然而，在必要情況下，台積電
將協助客戶確保產品如期交付， 暗示若 Intel 的 EMIB-T 製程進度落後，台積
電有望進一步提升市占率。部分投資人可能擔憂台積電導入 ASML High -NA
設備的時程較競爭對手晚兩年，進而影響其技術領先地位。但我們持不同看
法，採用最新設備並不必然代表技術領先。若 在 2030 年前台積電能在不投
資昂貴設備的情況下達到相近的製程效能，反而 有望取得相較同業更顯著的
成本優勢。 
投資建議  
儘管 3Q26 財報符合預期，且我們預估 4Q26 表現將更為強勁，但在市場預
期極高下，投資人仍可能對短期毛利率走勢有所疑慮。考量未來的成長動能
及穩健的執行能力，我們建議投資人趁股價回檔時逢低布局。我們僅微幅調
整獲利預估， 但隨評價基準遞延至 2028 年，我們 將目標價上調至 3,650
元，仍基於景氣上行週期平均的 20 倍本益比，維持「增加持股」評等 。 
投資風險  
全球景氣不確定性；積極擴產導致供給過剩。  
增加持股‧維持

台灣 台積電

十月 15, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 2

法說會重點數據 
 4Q26 財測： 營收預估為 523 億至 543 億美元，以中間值計算季增
14%；毛利率 65-67%，營益率 56.5- 58.5%。 
 2026 年展望：全年營收成長預估上調至年增 44%，維持資本支出 600
億至 640 億美元。  
 毛利率稀釋：海外擴產預計稀釋 2026 年毛利率 2 個百分點，未來幾年
稀釋幅度將擴大至 3-4 個百分點。  
 產能規劃：  
 N2 製程家族產能預計於 2027 年增加至兩倍以上。  
 台南新增 N3 晶圓廠預計於 1H27 量產，另一座 N3 晶圓廠則規劃於
1H28 量產。 
 亞利桑那州 N3 二廠已完成廠房建設，目前正進行設備搬入及安
裝，預計於 3Q27 量產，略早於原訂時程。  
 日本 N3 製程預計於 2028 年量產。

2028 年量產。

ESG 圖表定義  
項目 定義 說明 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括
為滿足能源使用而生產的電力(公用事業為出售而生產) 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下

購買之再生能源 
Primary再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者(太陽能、風能、水能、生
物質能、地熱能)之量 
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源

再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例  
CO2約當排放量 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

CO2約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2與 CO2約當排放量(公噸)銷售(百萬元)比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量(範圍 1排放量) 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳(CO2)、甲烷(CH4)、一氧化二氮(N2O)、氫氟碳化物(HFCS)、全氟化合物
(PFCS)、六氟化硫(SF6)、三氟化氮(NF3)

廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄
物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內

廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物

總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮

環保支出 
環保支出總金額 
- 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處
置、處理、衛生、清理支出

員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因(自願或非自願)離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失
能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 = (離開之員工/平均員工人數)*100 
- 平均員工人數 = (本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數)/2 
- 本財年末員工數 = 上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數

女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100

女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100

教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練(如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展) 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1天 = 8小時工作

每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數  
資料來源：Refinitiv，凱基

水冷機櫃與機殼本業同步走升 
 2Q26 本業穩健成長 ，EPS 1.41 元：晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，
年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至 20.5%，主要是因客戶
Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟階段，客戶
持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。 
 預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26 開始爬升：展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年
增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為傳統旺季，加上
VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，出貨
量將逐步上升 ， 隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，
預期機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案
有 8 至 9 成聚焦機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11
系統整合業務營收將進一步提升。 
 維持買進，目標價 140 元：投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機
殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進一步成長，且公司已成功切入
Meta 供應鏈，並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認證，可直接承接
Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟銘電
水冷相關業務比重持續提升 ， 使2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以
上 ， 投顧預估2026 年 EPS 6.13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元 ， 維持買進 ，
目標價 140 元。

個股報告

晟銘電 為伺服器機殼大廠，目前
切入 IT 機櫃、水冷機櫃、水冷搭
配的分歧管 
 晟銘電子科技股份有限公司成立於 1976 年 6 月 ， 為台灣伺服器機殼OEM/ODM
製造商， 公司主要銷售電腦機殼及伺服器機殼 ，其用途為桌上型電腦及伺服器等
各項組件之安裝防護設備 ， 近年來公司積極跨足散熱領域並研發設計出液冷散熱
的浸沒式散熱產品。於 2024 年公司推出多款液冷機櫃及液冷散熱系統。晟銘電
提供全球客戶迅速且彈性的 OEM 及 ODM 生產服務。2026 年 1~8 月主要產品
營收占比，伺服器 73%、L11 系統整合 18%、Tooling 6%、DT 3%。

Exhibit 3: 產品組合

Sources : Company、MasterLink

2Q26 本業穩健成長，EPS 1.41
元 
 晟銘電 2Q26 營收 32.19 億元，年增 22.5%，毛利率則從前期 21.1%降至
20.5%，主要是因客戶 Storage 相關產品出貨增加，然 Storage 產品已進入成熟
階段，客戶持續每季議價，2Q26 稅後淨利為 2.9 億元，EPS 1.41 元。

預期 3Q26 為營運谷底，4Q26
開始爬升 
 展望 26H2，3Q26 營收年增 16%，估計 3Q26 稅後 EPS 1.49 元。4Q 通常為
傳統旺季，加上 VR200 也會在 4Q26 陸續加入量產，而晟銘電也有參與其中，
出貨量將逐步上升，隨著 VR200 及 ASIC 等新平台陸續進入放量階段，預期
機殼與 Sidecar 出貨量將同步提升。此外，2026 年 ODM 專案有 8 至 9 成聚焦
機櫃業務，涵蓋 OCP、EIA 以及 MGX，預期 L11 系統整合業務營收將進一步
提升。

今年前八月 L11 系統組裝營收占比已達 18％ ， 而明年L11 系統組裝營收貢獻還
會持續提升。公司在機櫃產品線已相當完整，還可提供配套的水冷配件，機箱
基本上是跟隨著輝達的腳步走 ， 包括GB300、VR200 等標準品機箱都已經開好
模具。海外產能布局，公司目前生產基地仍以中國大陸為主，其中東莞為主要
生產基地，另於寧波設有產能，產品以機殼、機櫃為主，東莞廠自動烤漆線預
計今年底前完成，一天可以完成60 個機櫃烤漆；台灣中壢則主要負責打樣及水
冷產品研發，部分零組件仍由中國生產後運送至中壢進行後段組裝。泰國一期
廠已完成建置並具備生產條件 ， 二期則持續進行設備調校 ， 主要導入烤漆產線，
預計 4Q26 開始試產，後續待客戶完成稽核及認證後逐步量產。泰國二期的烤

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (115)金管投顧新字第 016 號         Page 3 of 5 
個股報告 
漆設備規格除可支援機殼外，亦可延伸至機櫃產品，有助未來將部分機櫃製程
移往海外。今年中壢廠、泰國廠都有新產能加入，而明年資本支出大致也會維
持在大約 7～8 億元。

維持買進，目標價 140 元 
 投顧認為晟銘電 4Q26 起受惠 VR200 機殼與 ASIC 放量，2027 年營運可望進
一步成長 ， 且公司已成功切入Meta 供應鏈 ， 並在冷卻機櫃與機構件取得關鍵認
證，可直接承接Meta 釋出的巨額硬體訂單，且在液冷架構成為主流帶動下，晟
銘電水冷相關業務比重持續提升，使 2026 年整體毛利率有望升到 20%以上，
投顧預估 2026 年 EPS 6. 13 元，2027 年 EPS 9.4 元，維持買進，目標價 140
元。(15x2027EPS)

風險提示

1) 客戶機箱導入狀況。 
2) L11 系統組裝發展狀況。

營收走勢圖

研究員/游周翰 Jeffery.Yu@uni-psg.com

研究部重申華星光買進建議，主要考量：(1)管理層維持下半年營運優
於上半年展望，受惠 800G DCI 及 CW Laser 需求持續成長，4Q26 單月
營收有望挑戰 6 億元；(2)隨晶粒稼動率及良率持續改善，研究部上修
2027 年晶粒出貨量預估，加上 EML 等產品逐步放量，有望帶動產品組
合優化及獲利能力提升。綜上所述，研究部預估華星光 2026/2027 年
營收，由原先 60.6 億元/119.8 億元調整為 58.4 億元/120.8 億元
(+33%/107%YoY)，EPS 由原先 7.69 元/15.88 元調整為 7.5 元/16.35
元。考量2027 年產能擴充及相關產品出貨成長，營收與獲利有望呈現
倍數成長，維持目標價 800 元的買進建議。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26營收優於研究部預期，惟受匯率影響EPS僅符合預期： 
華星光於 10/14 公 佈 自 結 ，9 月營收 5.75 億元（ +5.3%MoM 、
+97.8%YoY） ，稅後淨利1.03億元，稅後淨利率達17.9%，EPS達
0.72元。相較於8月回升，除了匯率回穩外，亦受到營收規模放
大所影響 。 累計3Q26營收為16.29億元 （+26.4%QoQ，+51.4%YoY），
稅後淨利達3.26億元，EPS為2.28元，創歷史新高。若依研究部
過去費用率及業外假設回推，3Q26毛利率約28-29%（約+6ppts 
QoQ） ，主要受惠先前財稅認列時間差回補、產能利用率提升及
產品組合優化 。 整體而言 ，3Q26營收優於研究部預期16.19億元 ，
EPS僅符合預期2.26元 ， 推測主因受到匯率所影響毛利率及業外
損益。

表一、3Q26 研究部預估與實際差異 
單位：百萬元 實際 研究部原估 差異

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

2. 下半年營運優於上半年，惟毛利率受新產品良率等因素影響 ： 
展望4Q26， 管理層維持2H26營運優於1H26， 除了既有DCI產品持
續成長外，CW Laser需求持續提升、800G DCI進入批量生產，
以及EML與高增益APD開始貢獻營收，有望帶動整體營運向上，
單月營收有望挑戰6億元 。 近期經研究部通路訪查 ，800GZR出貨
量略低於原先預估，因此研究部下修 4Q26出貨量從75K下修至
55K，4Q26營收從原先預估19.6億元下修至17.31億元。此外考
量800GZR毛利率因良率及協助客戶購買稀釋毛利率不如原先研
究部預期的情況下，將EPS從2.51元下修至2.29元。

3. 隨產能擴充，營收獲利將持續成長： 
展望未來，在2027年產能倍增情況下，營收規模有望呈現倍數
成長。晶粒方面，除了既有的CW Laser產能擴充外，亦增加EML
晶粒產能，使公司在InP產品線有多種產品分散風險。此外，近
期經供應鏈訪查，晶粒稼動率及良率提升下，有望使整體晶粒
出貨量優於原先預期。因此研究部預估2027年整體晶粒出貨量
從4700萬顆上修至5000萬顆。綜上所述，研究部預估營收2027
年營收從119.8億元上修至120.8億元 ， EPS從15.88元微幅上修
至16.35元。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

供 專 屬 之 投 資 建 議 。 文 中 所 載 資 訊 或 任 何 意 見 ， 不 構 成 任 何 買 賣 有 價 證 券 或 其 他 投
資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投
資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資
料 來 源 ， 但 概 不 以 明 示 或 默 示 的 方 式 ， 對 資 料 之 準 確 性 、 完 整 性 或 正 確 性 作 出 任 何
陳 述 或 保 證 。 本 投 資 報 告 載 述 意 見 進 行 更 改 與 撤 回 並 不 另 行 通 知 。 本 投 資 報 告 並 非
（且不應 解 釋 為 ）在 任 何 司 法 管 轄 區 內 ， 任 何 非 依 法 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 人 士 或
公 司 ， 為 於 該 管 轄 區 內 從 事 證 券 經 紀 或 交 易 之 遊 說 。 本 投 資 報 告 內 容 屬 統 一 投 顧 之

## Document 270: Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Fed builds credibility with 
hike; one more likely in Dec 
Summary 
Event: The US Federal Reserve hiked its key interest rate by 
25bps to 3.75-4.0%, in a unanimous decision. This marks the 
first hike since 2023, reversing the easing cycle that had 
taken rates down from 4.25% in September 2025 to a trough 
of 3.75% by December 2025. The revised projections 
indicate the Fed expects to raise rates by an additional 25bps 
this year, hold rates in 2027, and cut 25bps in both 2028 and 
2029. It upgraded the long-run rate slightly to 3.2%.  
Market reaction: The US yield curve mildly flattened , while 
the S&P500 index fell 0.5%. The policy rate sensitive US 2 -
year government bond yield jumped 7 bps to 4. 74% as 
markets factored in  three more 25bps hikes by June 2027 . 
The US 10-year yield rose 2bps to 5.02%, its highest since 
2007, while the 30-year yield was little changed. The USD 
index (DXY) rose 0.6%, breaking past its 50- and 100-DMAs. 
Investment strategy: Today’s move realises what we had 
been flagging in recent weeks i.e. the rising risk of a Fed rate 
hike following Fed Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole speech and 
recent economic data prints, namely: a pick-up in job creation 
and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in oil 
prices due to the re-escalation of the Middle East conflict.  
This hike helps the Fed rebuild credibility as an inflation 
fighter. Given the unanimous decision to hike rates this week, 
we believe there will likely be one more hike before the end 
of this year, and one more in H1 2027 as we expect robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated.  That scenario would still be less 
than the three more rate hikes money markets are pricing 
after the latest Fed move. We believe policy rates can return 
to the 4.0 –4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility of 
cuts in H2 2027  as the impact of tariffs and high oil prices 
start to fade towards the second half of 2027.   
Today’s refreshed Fed policy rate path is mildly bullish for the 
USD index; so we revise up our 3 -month target to 100. We 
also revise up our 3-month 10-year yield target to 5.0-5.25%.  
 The USD rose, while stocks fell after the Fed hiked rates 
and signalled one more rate hike this year; ultra-long 
bonds rose slightly as the Fed rebuild its inflation-
fighting credentials 
Asset performance: 15-Sep close to 16-Sep close

2026-27 and inflation projection for 2026; however, it 
still sees inflation cooling over the next two years

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 17 September 2026

Backdrop to the rate hike 
In hiking rates, Fed Chair Warsh reaffirmed the inflation 
threat to the US economy. US headline inflation accelerated 
to 0.4% m/m in August and stayed elevated on an annualised 
basis at 3.4% y/y. Energy prices , driven by the Middle East 
conflict, were a primary contributor. Oil prices have extended 
gains since the August inflation report, surging past USD 
100/bbl. Meanwhile, core inflation rose 0.3% m/m in August, 
beating estimates for a 0.2% rise . Also, US producer prices 
jumped 0.4% m/m in August, the most since May, and 5.4%  
y/y, further reinforcing the inflationary backdrop. 
The inflation report follow ed a stronger -than-expected jobs 
report for August. US monthly job creation has surged to an 
average 80,000 this year, from 10,000, tightening a job 
market that already faces a supply crunch from the Trump 
administration’s clampdown against immigration.  The 
combination of an acceleration in job creation and inflation 
over the summer, combined with a rebound in oil prices, 
supported our expectation for a Fed rate hike this week.  
Removing “a dose of accommodation” 
Warsh framed the decision to hike as removing "a dose of 
accommodation", rather than the start of an aggressive rate 
hiking cycle, and we agree with that characterisation. The aim 
is to bring financial and credit conditions more in line with the 
Fed's price stability objectives. To quote Warsh: “Our 
predominant focus is on the price stability side of our 
mandate. The plain fact is that inflation is too high, and has 
been for too long….This  summer’s inflation readings do not 
tell me that underlying trends have meaningfully improved.” 
He added that many categories in the last consumer and 
producer inflation reports are still showing price increases 
above 3% on a six- and 12-month basis.  
The unanimous vote to hike rates, contrasts with a 9 -3 split 
vote in July to hold rates. When combined with the pencilling 
in of one more hike by December, this suggests a majority of 
Fed policymakers have turned more hawkish over the 
summer. In doing so, they have helped rebuild the Fed’s 
inflation-fighting credentials and its independence against 
President Trump’s pressures to cut rates. This explains why 
long-end bond yields were stable after this week’s hike. 
Nevertheless, Warsh provided little guidance on the future 
rate path, as he held what was reportedly the shortest post -
meeting press conference on record for a Fed chair . This is 
consistent with his stated preference for less central bank 
communication. We expect more clarity once the Fed task 
forces submit their reports by year end. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist

last year, tightening a job market already facing a 
potential crunch from an immigration clampdown

## Document 287: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Bond Yields Ease

Source PDF: all report pdf/GLOBAL MARKETS CALL_ Bond Yields Ease.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond Yields Ease
Date:    3 hours ago
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond Yields Ease

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
GLOBAL MARKETS CALL: Bond
Yields Ease
By Ed Yardeni & Toby Hearst • 25 Oct 2026
View in browser
Photo by Jeffrey Hamilton / Unsplash

Bond yields eased in most major markets last week, but France was the
glaring exception. In stocks, the global AI selloff kept hitting the equities
markets that had gained the most this year, putting the US back ahead of
the All Country World ex-US ytd. That supports our downgrade last week
of Go Global to market weight, though the fundamental case for overseas
stocks remains strong. Here's more:
I. Global Bonds

### Page 2

Yields fell in five of the eight major bond markets we track (chart). The US
10-year Treasury yield fell to 5.00%. France's yield jumped 24bps to
5.02%, slightly surpassing the US’s. Yields also fell in the UK, Australia,
Germany, and Japan.
The spread between French and German 10-year yields widened 31bps
last week to 167.9bps (chart). Last week, we wrote that contagion from
France had eased for now. It’s back. Italy’s spread widened to 123.9bps

from 106.6bps. Greece’s widened to 105.0bps from 90.8bps. Italy’s 10-
year bond now yields 44bps less than France’s.
II. Global Stocks
Brazil remains at the top of the country ETF rankings mtd, up 14.3% in
dollars (chart). South Korea remains at the bottom, down 17.2%. The US

### Page 3

is down just 0.3%, while the All Country World ex-US ETF is down 2.8%.
Go Global no longer leads Stay Home this year. The US ETF is up 11.4%
ytd, ahead of the All Country World ex-US ETF's 9.5% (chart). Taiwan
(65.1%) and South Korea (55.6%) still top the ytd rankings, followed by
Brazil (34.1%). China (-15.6%) and Indonesia (-43.3%) are at the bottom.

### Page 4

III. Global Earnings & Valuations
The US MSCI's share of the All Country World MSCI's market cap has
edged up to 65.0% (chart). The Emerging Markets (EM) MSCI’s share
slipped to 11.7% from 12.1% last week.
Yet the EM MSCI now accounts for 20.2% of the All Country World MSCI’s
forward earnings (chart). That’s more than Europe’s 15.0%. It’s the first

### Page 5

time that EM has overtaken Europe since the data began in 1995. The US
accounts for 54.4% of forward earnings, well below its market-cap share.
That mismatch shows up in valuations. The US MSCI’s forward P/E is
19.2 versus 12.2 for the All Country World ex-US (chart). The ex-US
multiple has dropped from 15.7 in January even as its forward earnings
soared. The EM MSCI trades at just 9.0.
IV. In Focus
(1) Brazil. Brazil’s MSCI stock price index is up 22.2% ytd in local

currency and 31.0% in dollars (chart). The real has gained 8.0% against
the dollar this year. In dollar terms, the index remains less than half its
2008 peak. Stocks rallied after Senator Flávio Bolsonaro took a surprise
lead in the October 4 first round. He held a narrow edge in the final polls

### Page 6

before the October 25 runoff against President Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva. A
Bloomberg poll released on October 19 gave Bolsonaro 51.1% of valid
votes against 48.9% for Lula.
Valuations aren’t stretched. Brazil’s forward P/E is 9.2 (chart). That’s
below its 2025 high of 10.7. The catch is that analysts expect earnings to
grow 44.6% this year but just 0.6% in 2027.
Brazil’s central bank has room to ease. It has lowered the Selic rate to
13.75% from a peak of 15.00% (chart). Inflation was 4.6% in September.

That leaves a real policy rate of about 9%.

### Page 7

(2) China. China’s property bust is deepening. The Shenzhen Real Estate
Stock Price Index fell to 953.55, breaking below the floor that had held
since 2012 (chart). Last week, we wrote that we would worry if it did that.
Now we are worried. Real estate investment is down 20.6% through the
first nine months of the year. Exports continue to carry the economy.
Beijing’s stabilization efforts haven’t stopped the selling. The China ETF
(FXI) fell 4.5% last week. Chinese technology stocks have missed the AI

rally that lifted US tech (chart). The China Technology ETF (CQQQ) is at
40.26, far below its 2021 peak.

### Page 8

China’s bond market confirms the deflationary picture. The 10-year
government bond yield is down to 1.62% (chart). It’s one of only two major
yields that have fallen this year. China’s yield is now well below Japan’s
2.80%.

## Document 343: STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank
Research

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.

CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?
Probably more relevant than having the stablecoin
market cap here I would say.

### Page 7

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
Shows projected years until crypto merchant transactions reach Visa scale.

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
GEOPOLITICS: Why stablecoins are a "Genius" idea for the US
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, US Treasury, Tether July 2026 report. Shows Major foreign holders of Treasury Securities.
Treasury data from June 2026.
Can sustain USD dominance
– 99% of stablecoins (by market cap) are USD-pegged.
– Stablecoins function primarily as a digital version of dollars

moving over blockchain rails, extending the dollar’s reach into
new digital environments.
– Reinforces dollar savings: The more firms invoice and transact in USD,
the more they save in USD assets. Stablecoins help preserve this
feedback loop by keeping businesses and individuals in dollar
payment rails.
Can support US debt, given share held by foreigners has fallen from
>50% in early 2000s to ~30% in early 2026.
– Stablecoin issuers total holdings: ~$170bn according to Allium x

Citizen.
– Tether: ~$141bn in direct and indirect exposure to US T-bills.
– Impact: BIS shows how $3.5bn inflow into dollar-backed stablecoins
lowers 3m T-bill yields by 2.5-3.5bp under normal conditions. Effect
more than doubles in periods of T-bill shortages.
– Forecasts: T-bills holding by stablecoin issuers could reach ~$1trn by
2028 (Treasury forecasts).
– Recent Fed SF research places it closer to $400bn by the end
of 2030, lower than the US government’s financing needs.

Stablecoin issuers’  demand for US debt could counterweight declining demand from China
Tether competes with Saudi Arabia and South Korea as major holders of US Treasuries
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, US Treasury Data. Shows Treasury
holdings, stablecoin issuers vs China.

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
US GENIUS ACT: Debate on rules around payment stablecoins one year on
GENIUS provisions
– Strict issuer requirements: Issuers can be private, non-bank entity, but must
meet strict federal or state requirements.
– Reserves: Requires 1:1 backing with US cash, 93 days T-bills, repos/reverse
repos, or money market funds holding those assets.
– AML/KYC/BSA compliance obligations to stablecoin issuers, aligning them

exams, and strong internal controls.
Next steps
– GENIUS takes effect no later than 18 Jan 2027, or 120 days after final
implementing regulations are issued.
– Ongoing pushback from US banks as to whether GENIUS ban on yield-
bearing stablecoins should extend to affiliate or third-party arrangements,
given many users do not hold stablecoins directly with the issuer.
– CLARITY ACT failed in Sep because of disagreements on how
exchanges/broader crypto market structure should handle stablecoins.

– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
There are two ways stablecoin yield can be paid; ongoing debate over the three-
party model
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Congressional Research Service.

RISKS: Still hotly discussed amongst policymakers and financial institutions
Bank liquidity risk
Loss of stable funding source for banks from deposit outflow (not offset by
partial flowback from stablecoin issuers’ reserve deposit)
System stability risk
Stablecoins can fail to maintain their $1 value during times of stress. Evidence shows
that USDC and Tether actually trade within 1% of their dollar value but prices can

diverge sharply during stress episodes.
Run on stablecoin issuer(s) in market stress / trust scenario triggering fire sales of
reserve assets and/or deposit withdrawals.

Backing Assets*
• 100% backing assets in USD cash or short-term
Treasuries (≤93-day T-bills).
• Non-significant EMTs –At least 30% reserves in

• Significant EMTs - At least 60% of the reserves in

• Systemic stablecoins – 30% unremunerated BoE
deposits vs 70% in UK sovereign debt (HQLA)
• Non-systemic – Full 1:1 backing in HQLR assets,
minimum 5% in on demand deposits.
UK
Multi-issuance
Stance
• Rules are still being fine-tuned; no specific focus
yet on multi-issuance.
• Multi-issuance under active review; EC considers multi-
issuance permissible, an equivalence-based framework for

EU/third-country arrangements is under legislative
consideration.
• BoE considers multi-issuance unsuitable for systemic
UK use. Preference for single-issuance models backed
by single reserve (incl. Non-GPB denom. stablecoins) .
UK Cryptoasset Regime (FCA)
UK Systemic Stablecoins Regime (BoE)
Eligible Issuers
• Bank subsidiaries, federal nonbank licensees, or
state issuers with federal registration, with
enhanced oversight for >$10bn circulation.

• EMTs: EU-authorised credit institutions or authorised
electronic money institutions. Restrictions apply to
non-EU currency EMTs (exceeding MiCAR
thresholds) as a means of exchange
• FCA-authorised firms for non-systemic stablecoins,
• Systemic issuers to be designated by HM Treasury
and jointly regulated under BoE/FCA oversight
Balance Sheet
Treatment
• Stablecoins treated as reserve-backed digital
payment assets, segregated in bankruptcy-remote

accounts and treated as cash equivalents
• EMTs are fiat-referenced cryptoassets subject to
MiCAR and e-money requirements
• (OCC/FDIC) Mandates prudential capital buffers
tailored to issuer risk profile.
• (Fed) Framework consistent with bank requirements
• EMI issuers: minimum own funds of 2% of EMT
supply; 3% for significant EMTs. Significant issuers
subject to enhanced risk-management requirements
• Systemic: 6 months operating expenses/largest

plausible loss event
• Non-systemic: 1% av. stablecoins in circulation

Charts of the week: No capitulation

A hotter-than-expected US inflation report triggered a pullback in risk assets, but there are few signs of capitulation so far 
US core and supercore inflation; US inflation breadth*    Our ‘Fear and Greed’ indicator

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *share of US consumer inflation components rising more than 3%

Editorial 
Staying defensive and diversified  
Strategy summary:  Two weeks ago, we turned defensive 
towards equities, locking in semiconductor equity gains amid 
elevated bond volatility, narrowing market breadth and political 
uncertainty before the US mid -term elections. The near -term 
outlook reflected a less favourable risk-reward balance. Since 
we turned defensive , the Nasdaq 100 index has recorded its 
biggest weekly decline in three months as September’s hotter-
than-expected US inflation report briefly lifted bond yields. 
We have extended our defensive stance this week, 
downgrading US Communication Services equities to Neutral 
and upgrading US Healthcare to Overweight. We also closed 
our opportunistic bullish Communication Services idea and 
initiated a bullish idea on Healthcare, where earnings visibility 
and defensive characteristics appear more attractive. In fixed 
income, we see opportunities in US 10-year government bonds. 
We await clearer AI profit margins and spending guidance, 
broader earnings growth or lower bond and equity market 
volatility before turning positive on equities. Until then, 
diversification and disciplined entry levels remain important. 
Hot US inflation triggers equity pullback; not hot enough 
for an October Fed hike:  Risk assets pulled back after 
September’s hotter-than-expected inflation data (headline 3.7% 
y/y; core 2.6% y/y). Higher gasoline and diesel prices were the 
main drivers, but passthrough appeared in airline fares, used 
cars and transportation services, showing pressures were not 
confined to energy. Almost half of inflation components are still 
rising above 3%, a measure recently cited by Fed Chair Warsh. 
Producer inflation also beat estimates. The reports revive the 
possibility of an October Fed rate hike, but we expect a divided 
Fed to weigh it against September’s soft jobs report and 
narrowly hold this month. A December hike appears more likely 
if inflation remains firm . Although Fed Chair Warsh did not 
comment on policy at an IMF speech this week, New York Fed 
chief Williams said he saw no need to hike in 2027. Our base 
case remains two further hikes by June 2027. 
Limited equity pullback so far.  Equities fell sharply after the 
US inflation report, but the pullback is limited. The Nasdaq 100 
is 4.5% below its record high, while the S&P 500 remains close 
to all -time highs despite the latest decline. The S&P 500 
volatility index (VIX) has risen from below 15 to 1 8.2, still well 
under the 25 –30 range typically associated with capitulation 
and forced selling. Meanwhile, US government bond volatility 
(MOVE) remains elevated after surging since September.  Our 
stock-bond quant model turned more negativ e (-2) this week, 
signalling elevated equity volatility in the near term.  
When to turn more constructive on stocks?  We would turn 
more constructive when capitulation signals align: the Nasdaq 
100 approaches a 10% drawdown, the VIX rises to 25 –30 and 
our ‘Fear and Greed’ index  enters “extreme fear”. Greater 
confidence would also require easing concerns about AI 
spending and technology firms’ exposure to neocloud revenue, 
lower bond volatility and our stock-bond model turning positive 
again. Until these conditions emerge, volatility may remain 
elevated, favouring a defensive equity stance. Political risk 
should ease after the US mid -term elections. We continue to 
seek selective opportunities following excessive corrections. 
US financials remain a preferred sector after Q3 earnings:  
US large banks’ earnings were mixed, but underlying trends 
remain encouraging. Healthy loan and deposit growth supports 
net interest income, while resilient consumer spending, wealth 
management activity and a solid M&A and refinancing pipeline 
strengthen the outlook. Credit risks appear manageable . We 
remain constructive and favour stronger, diversified franchises. 
US government bonds offer value as yields spike:  The 
reversal in US government bond yields after their initial inflation-
driven spike suggests limited further upside. The 10 -year yield 
briefly reached 5.32% before slumping as falling equities 
prompted short-covering. We expect a near -term 5.00–5.25% 
range and view moves above 5.25% as attractive entry points 
for our opportunistic 10-year government bond idea. Structural 
pressures, including term premium and increasing supply to 
fund fiscal deficits and AI investments, prevent us from turning 
outright bullish on longer maturities. We, therefore, continue to 
prefer 3–7-year maturities in broader bond allocations.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions 
  How does the September 2026 US Consumer Price Index 
(CPI) report alter your view on the USD and bond yields?   
Our view: The hotter-than-expected CPI print raises the odds of 
an October Fed hike, though we still expect a hold followed by 
a rate hike in December. In the near term, we maintain our view 
that the US 10 -year government bond yield will range between 
5.00% and 5.25%, while  the USD will remain supported as 
markets reassess the likelihood of further Fed hikes. Additional 
gains would depend partly on markets pricing a faster or more 
prolonged tightening cycle, while clearer signs of an 
approaching policy rate peak could see the USD ease back. 
Rationale: September US consumer inflation  came in above 
consensus across the board – headline CPI rose 3.7% y/y (vs. 3.6% 
estimate), and core CPI rose 2.6% y/y (vs. 2.5% estimate) – marking 
the fastest headline acceleration in five months. This shifts the 
balance of risks modestly hawkish . Unsurprisingly, the market -
implied probability of an October Fed hike rose over 50% overnight 
but closed the day at 32.7% – 9ppt higher than Tuesday’s close. 
We believe a split Fed will weigh this inflation report against the soft 
September jobs report and narrowly vote to keep rates on hold this 
month. Our base case remains for two more Fed hikes  by June 
2027. A December rate hike  now looks more like a done deal, 
especially as persistently higher oil prices keep headline and core 
inflation elevated for at least the next few months. Any re-escalation 
of the Middle East conflict after the  US mid-term elections would 
exert pressure on the Fed, although we believe Republicans would  
show greater restraint in US foreign action if they suffer steep Senate 
losses, which looks increasingly likely. 
The intra-day price action in US government bonds was instructive. 
The US 10-year yield briefly spiked to 5.32% on the print before 
closing materially lower, as pre -existing bearish positioning was 
unwound and Fed speakers (Barkin, Goolsbee) stopped well short 
of signalling an imminent move. This dynamic reinforces our view 
that the technical selling pressure driving yields towards the upper 
end of our range is fading. Structural drivers – term premium, fiscal 
supply concerns and ‘higher for longer’ Fed repricing – remain intact 
and keep us from turning outright bullish on duration. However, our 
opportunistic bullish idea in US 10-year government bonds initiated 
at September-end remains, as we continue to view spikes in US 10-
year yields over 5.25% as a good risk-reward entry. From a longer-
term portfolio standpoint, we continue to favour the 3-7-year tenor. 
On FX, elevated US government bond yields should sustain the 
USD’s interest rate appeal. However, expected hikes alone will not 
drive further appreciation. Stronger data or firmer Fed guidance 
would provide fresh support, while evidence that tightening is 
nearing completion would temper the USD’s strength. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

rangebound between 102 and 103, supported by 
elevated US government bond yields

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What are your key takeaways from US bank earnings? 
Our view: Q3 2026 earnings results from major US banks have 
been mixed, but underlying trends remain encouraging. Healthy 
loan and deposit growth, resilient consumer spending and 
wealth management activity continue to support the sector. 
Credit risks remain manageable. We remain constructive on the 
US financial sector, favouring stronger, diversified franchises. 
Rationale: Healthy loan and deposit growth continue to support net 
interest income (NII), hinting that the US economy remains resilient. 
Capital markets revenue grew year -on-year (y/y) , but moderated 
sequentially after a very strong Q2 2026, although performance 
varied across banks. Management commentary points to a healthy 
deal pipeline , supported by M&A activity and debt refinancing 
demand, although execution will depend on market conditions. 
Provisioning and net charge -off trends were mixed across banks, 
with no clear signs of broad deterioration in credit quality. Firmer 
US inflation raises the risk of further monetary tightening. While 
higher rates can support asset yields, they can also increase 
funding costs, pressure borrowers and weigh on securities ’ 
valuations. We will continue to monitor lending demand, credit 
costs, NII trends, capital markets momentum and capital returns.  
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Can Big Tech’s earnings guidance sustain confidence in 
the AI investment cycle? 
Our view: We expect Big Tech’s AI spending to remain elevated, 
supporting our constructive AI capex outlook. However, we 
await guidance on the pace of capex growth and the 
sustainability of profit margins. 
Rationale: Semiconductor leaders’ latest company guidance points 
to supply constraints extending through 2028, supporting continued 
capacity expansion for compute . However, while AI capex is still 
likely to rise, y/y growth is expected to moderate  from c.100% in 
Q3 2026 to c.20% in Q3 2027. This normalisation in growth could 
raise concerns about the AI investment cycle’s durability.  
The Nasdaq-100 Index has rallied alongside accelerating AI capex. 
Slower capex growth is likely to moderate semiconductor revenue 
growth, while hyperscalers continue to incur depreciation costs on 
past investments. The slower growth is already partially reflected in 
the Nasdaq -100’s valuation de -rating this year. While we remain 
constructive on the AI investment theme, we prefer to stay 
prudent in the near term and await Big Tech’s guidance for greater 
visibility into spending plans in H2 2027 and beyond. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would France face a credit rating downgrade by rating 
agencies this month? What might be the market implications? 
Our view: A rating downgrade is unlikely unless France’s 2027 
budget passes the National Assembly very soon with material 
changes to the proposal. We see French government bond 
yields remaining elevated relative to German Bund yields. 
Rationale: The French National Assembly began debating France’s 
2027 draft budget on 13 October 2026, with the first vote scheduled 
for 20 October. Near-term budget approval appears unlikely, as 
opposition parties have tabled motions to reject the proposal. The 
French Parliament has up to 70 days from the budget’s submission 
to complete the legislative process, although  its final adoption and 
the resolution of political challenges could extend into early 2027.  
Last October, two of the three major rating agencies downgraded 
France by one notch to the high-single-A category  with stable 
outlooks. The remaining agenc y rated France in the low-AA 
category with a negative outlook. As negative outlooks are typically 
resolved within 12 -18 months, agenc ies have time to await fiscal 
clarity before acting. We believe downgrade risks are largely priced 
in after the recent rise in yields, but volatility is likely to persist amid 
fiscal uncertainty and the approaching presidential election. Banks 
and domestically-focused issuers would face the greatest near-term 
pressure. However, we would view wider spreads as an opportunity 
to add exposure, particularly to French banks’ subordinated 
debt, given their stronger balance sheets and the fact that France 
contributes less than 50% of major French banks’ overall revenue. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Japanese banks? 
Our view: We are positive on Japanese banks despite the recent 
market correction, as the structural drivers of improving 
profitability and shareholder returns remain intact . While 
political risk and shifting BoJ rate hike odds could sustain near-
term volatility, we see the recent pullback as an opportunity to 
build selective exposure to Japanese bank stocks. 
Rationale: Japan’s gradual monetary policy normalisation  
should support banks’ NII as floating-rate loans reprice and maturing 
assets are reinvested at higher yields. Healthy corporate lending 
demand, improving capital efficiency and continued share buybacks 
provide further support . While uncertainty over the pace of BoJ 
tightening could weigh on sentiment, we see gradual interest rate 
normalisation as supportive of medium-term bank profitability.  
Key risks  include rising deposit costs, mark -to-market losses on 
bond portfolios and a disorderly increase in Japanese government 
bond yields. We continue to favour well-capitalised banks  with 
strong domestic deposit franchises and improving returns on equity. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 16 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Could NZD/USD rebound from its one-year trough? If so, 
how far could it recover?  
Our view: New Zealand’s improving domestic conditions, 
persistent inflation and technical support provide a basis for 
NZD/USD’s initial recovery towards the 0.57 level, although 
elevated US bond yields could slow its progress. 
Rationale: In Q3, New Zealand’s  seasonally-adjusted business 
confidence reading rose to a net 40% of firms expecting the economy 
to improve, up from net 14% in Q2 . Hiring intentions improved, and 
filled jobs rose 0.2% m/m in August. These developments suggest 
domestic conditions remain supportive for the NZD.  
New Zealand’s above-target inflation keeps further RBNZ tightening 
in focus. The 22 October consumer inflation data release and the 
28 October RBNZ policy meeting are key catalysts: persistent price 
pressures a nd improving demand are expected to prompt firmer 
policy guidance and strengthen the NZD’s interest-rate appeal. 
NZD/USD’s position near the lower boundary of its downtrend 
channel suggests scope for a technical rebound. We see 0.57 as 
the first recovery hurdle; a sustained break  above this level  would 
strengthen the case for further gains. Firmer US  consumer inflation 
remains a headwind, making a gradual recovery more plausible 
than a sharp reversal.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Can the proposed Russia -Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire ’ 
contain oil prices as Middle East risks rise? 
Our view: We expect escalating Middle East tensions to keep oil 
prices elevated . We  maintain our  three- and 12 -month West 
Texas Intermediate (WTI) oil price forecasts at USD 100/bbl and 
USD 80/bbl, respectively, and continue to favour US Treasury 
Inflation-Protected Securities (TIPS) as a portfolio hedge 
against persistent inflation risks. 
Rationale: The proposed Russia-Ukraine ‘energy ceasefire’ still 
looks some way off. In our view, even if an agreement is reached, it 
would do little to ease the supply risks building in the Middle East. 
Gulf oil exports have recovered to above 80% of pre-conflict levels 
in recent weeks, helping moderate the pace of price increases. 
However, the recovery remains vulnerable to disruptions, particularly 
following the latest Houthi attacks on Saudi energy infrastructure . 
Diesel and jet fuel remain under the most pressure , with Gulf 
exports of these fuels in September  at only around 60% of pre -
conflict levels. A sustained disruption to major Saudi production or 
export infrastructure remains the principal upside risk to oil prices. 
—  Anthony Naab, Investment Strategist

### Page 3

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Diplomacy appears firmly back in play. The past month saw continued fighting and renewed diplomacy.
Reporting suggests that the US is prepared to extend troop deployments in the Middle East to 2027. Iran
targeted both US forces in the region and some tankers moving through the Strait, but has been unable to
prevent a significant rebound in crude flows through Hormuz. On 22 SEP, talks were mediated by Qatar,

Pakistan and Egypt, and on 24 SEP, Iran presented the US with a seven-day roadmap to a ceasefire leading
to a phased reopening of the Strait of Hormuz and a return to nuclear negotiations, conditional on a US
lifting of its blockade of Iran, sanctions relief and broader cessation of hostilities. The Trump
Administration rejected the proposal, but negotiations continue.
To watch will be whether October is the month the war transitions into a negotiated settlement, which

may hinge on whether Iran is willing to make some concessions on sanctions relief and the nuclear
question, and whether Trump is keen to demonstrate that the war has produced a political and economic
result in the final weeks before the 3 NOV midterm elections. It is plausible that the current diplomatic
channel could produce a temporary de-escalation mechanism as long as there is pathway around the
broader nuclear issue.
Both sides will reconvene for trade talks in Beijing on 8-9 October, which the EU views as a deadline for

“tangible results”. Brussels remains focused on its rising trade deficit with China – which has reached a
record €1 billion per day – and the impact of Chinese goods on European industry. The bloc has vowed to
take measures if talks fail.
While both sides could achieve progress in talks, a lasting breakthrough appears improbable, setting the
stage for more trade tensions into 2027. New EU trade investigations, curbs on Chinese imports, and EU-
level instruments intended to address alleged Chinese overcapacity, are possible outcomes, to which

Beijing has stated it would respond.
China-EU trade talks could also impact China-US relations. Should talks with Brussels fall short of averting
increased European trade pressure, we assess that Beijing may further prioritise near-term US stability. See
Rules of Engagement: Mapping the Next Phase of China-US Ties for more.

### Page 4

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Latvia will elect all 100 members of the 15th Saeima. Latvia, which borders Russia and Belarus, has been
among the most vocal supporters of Ukraine. The next government will set the country’s course on
defence spending, Ukraine aid, sanctions enforcement and border security. The field is highly fragmented
with the governing parties lacking a majority in the polls. The fragmentation matters as a weakened and

multi-party scramble could mean a slower, more unstable coalition.
Brazil’s first electoral round is on 4 OCT, with a runoff on 25 OCT. This is a critical election as it will
decide whether Latin America’s biggest economy continues President Lula’s state-led social spending or
pivots to a more fiscally-orthodox agenda under Bolsonaro. Polls have gone from a Lula lead in the spring
to a statistical dead heat between Lula and Bolsonaro. A Brazilian Supreme Court crisis – which has

implicated Supreme Court Justices and politicians on both sides – is weighing on the race. Congressional
and gubernatorial races will also impact the governability of the country.
This election will also shape whether the country hedges between Washington and Beijing under Lula or
tilts toward closer US trade and security alignment under Bolsonaro with potential implications for
tariffs, BRICS+ cohesion, and Latin America’s broader positioning between the two powers.

The meetings will take place in Bangkok, bringing finance ministers, central bankers, policy-makers and
business leaders together. The agenda is expected to centre on global growth, financial stability, debt,
trade and investment, development finance and the impact of AI on jobs and productivity, with particular
attention given to mobilizing private capital. The key watchpoint will be the outlook for global growth
and inflation, debt vulnerabilities, the future of the international financial architecture and how policy-

makers respond to increasing fragmentation in trade and capital flows.

### Page 5

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Leaders are expected to focus heavily on Ukraine, European security and defence, competitiveness and
the EU’s economic position in a more contested global environment. They will also take forward
negotiations on the 2028-2034 EU long-term budget. They set themselves a soft deadline in June to get
the budget broadly locked down because missing it pushes ratification into 2027 and risks a funding gap

for beneficiaries from JAN 2028. To watch will be a genuine breakthrough on the multiannual framework
or more likely what “substantial” progress may look like. Ukraine funding is the other pressure point. This
meeting will be important for understanding the direction of European defence spending and industrial
policy, the next phase of budget negotiations and the EU response to growing external economic
pressure and strategic dependencies.
The Canadian province of Alberta will hold a referendum, which includes a question about the province’s

future within Canada. This is not an independence vote itself but asking voters to choose between
remaining a Canadian province and asking the Alberta government to begin the constitutional process
required for a future binding referendum on separation. Current polling suggests “remain” is comfortably
ahead. The significance is around potential renewed uncertainty around Canada’s federal cohesion,
energy policy, fiscal transfers and Alberta's relationship with Ottawa and Washington. Even a modest

agreement to begin a process towards secession would likely keep alive equalization grievances as a
structural fault line in Canadian federalism feeding into political risk.
.
The event’s theme focuses on “Advancing Alliances in a New Era.” It will tackle how governments and
businesses respond to a world where geopolitics, economic security, supply chains and technology are
increasingly intertwined. There is likely to be a strong focus on the future of the transatlantic

relationship, including how Europe adapts to a more demanding security environment. There will also be
discussions of tariffs, supply chain resilience, critical capabilities and the weaponization of economic
interdependence. It will align with Chancellor Merz’s efforts to ensure Europe actively shapes outcomes
around strategic autonomy and reducing China exposure in manufacturing.

### Page 6

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
The second summit will take place in Saudi Arabi, bringing EU and Gulf leaders together. The agenda is
expected to focus on regional security and the Middle East, energy and economic security, trade and
investment, infrastructure, technology and broader strategic cooperation. The security dimension will be
particularly salient given the current crisis in the region. Economically, the relationship is substantial: EU-

GCC trade reached €165.6bn in 2025.
To watch will be movement on the long-stalled EU-GCC free trade talks and visa-waiver requests from
Qatar and Saudi Arabia in addition to the extent of the focus on Gaza reconstruction, Iran and regional
security get relative to the economic agenda.
GCC states have consistently used these summits to press for clearer EU positioning on Middle East
conflicts in exchange for economic cooperation. The summit comes amid at a critical juncture where

Europe is recalibrating its external partnerships.
The Central Committee of the Communist Party of China will hold its Fifth Plenary Session in Beijing.
This will be one of the most important events on the country’s political calendar ahead of the 21st Party
Congress in late 2027.
The Plenum will focus on Party governance, and investors will scrutinise signals regarding leadership
changes and personnel reshuffles, institutional reforms, and political priorities for the next Congress.

While fifth plenums historically focus on economic planning, the development guidelines for the 15th
Five-Year Plan (2026–2030) were already adopted during the Fourth Plenum in OCT 2025, allowing the
upcoming session to focus on personnel and governance matters.

### Page 7

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026
Deutsche Bank
Research Institute
Elections will be held for the 26th Knesset, the first since the October 7th attacks and wars in Gaza,
Lebanon and Iran. This is the first Knesset since 1988 to actually complete a full four-year term without
early dissolution. Benjamim Netanyahu remains the dominant single figure but faces a fragmented field:
Naftali Bennett’s new “Together” party is polling as the main rival bloc, alongside Gadi Eisenkot’s Yashar,

Benny Gantz’s Blue and White, and the further right Ben Gvir and Smotrich factions.
Israel’s proportional system means the election is effectively about coalition formation. Key faultlines to
watch: the ultra-Orthodox draft-exemption fight, how the war’s legacy and hostage/reconstruction issues
in Gaza factor into campaign messaging, and whether a Netanyahu-led bloc can reassemble a working
majority or whether the Bennett/Eisenkot-style “anyone but Bibi” coalitions gain enough traction. The

outcome will shape Israel’s posture on critical regional issues, including Gaza reconstruction, Iran policy
and West Bank settlement activity.
This summit is shaping up as a particularly important juncture against a more ambitious push to redefine
the relationship. During Mark Carney’s SEP visit to Strasbourg, he called for a comprehensive strategic
alliance with Europe. European Commission President von der Leyen went further, proposing that Canada

become the EU’s first “associate member”.
The agenda is expected to centre on trade and economic security, defense, critical minerals, energy,
digital cooperation and supply-chain resilience, building on the EU-Canada security and Defense
Partnership in the EU’s SAFE defense initiative. The summit will be watched for concrete progress on a
digital trade agreement and broader economic cooperation.

Helen Belopolsky, Miha Hribernik | This Month in Geopolitics | October 2026

The foreign ministers’ meeting comes ahead of the December Leaders’ Summit in Miami under the US’
chairmanship of the G20 this year. Trump’s G20 year has organized around four expert tracks – trade,
innovation, energy and deregulation. The session is framed around economic prosperity, energy security
and technological innovation, a much more transactional focus than previous foreign ministerials, which

typically focused on geopolitical crisis management.
It will be a test of whether the G20 can sustain cooperation amid intensifying geopolitical fragmentation.
In addition, Russia has confirmed its intent to actively participate in the meeting. This would further
normalize Russia’s multilateral engagement. For Europe and other partners, a key question will be the
extent to which the G20 can be a forum for broader international cooperation.

### Page 1

日本

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和

2026年 10月 22日 全9 頁

「一度出せば足りる」統計へ 
公的統計のデジタル化を問う②－負担軽減の「実装」

政策調査部 主任研究員 溝端 幹雄

[要約] 
 前編で示した公的統計のデジタル化による負担軽減の効果を安定的に得るには、「制
度・法」「標準化」「府省の縦割り」「民間データの代表性・継続性」「品質保証」「コス
トとメリット還元」「機密・信頼」「人材・予算」という課題を、効果の段階（電子化
→削減→代替）に応じて計画的に手当てする必要がある。 
 これらの課題の背景には、各府省が所管分野の統計を作る分散型統計機構の下で、標
準化、データ連携、重複報告の解消を府省横断で進めることの難しさがある。現行の
第 IV 期の公的統計基本計画（以下、基本計画）が終了する 2027 年度を見据え、次期
の第 V 期基本計画に向けては、統計委員会や総務省の調整機能、各府省の実施体制を
含むガバナンス改革が問われる。 
 「機密・信頼」という主要な懸念は、制度だけでなく技術によっても軽減できる。秘密
計算・差分プライバシー・合成データ等のプライバシー強化技術（PETs ）については、
国連が公的統計向けのガイドを整備し、各国統計機関で実証や導入検討が進められて
いる。民間データの継続性リスクに対しては、提供のインセンティブ設計に加え、デ
ータアクセスや相互運用性のルールを定める欧州データ法・データスペース等の枠組
みも参考となる。ただし、欧州データ法に基づく公的機関の民間データ取得は、例外
的な必要性がある場合等に限定される。 
 第V期基本計画には、①once-only原則の明文化、②行政記録・民間データ利活用の制
度整備、③府省共通の報告基盤、④企業へのメリット還元、⑤ガバナンス・人材・予
算を柱に据え、費用便益と工程表を伴って位置づけるべきである。企業の報告者負担
の軽減を、公的統計の持続可能性確保の中核に据える好機である。

### Page 2

1

2 / 9

実現に向けた「課題」 
前編 1では、報告者負担を軽減するデジタル化の効果を、① 調査の電子化、② 行政記録情
報・企業保有データの連携による調査自体の削減、③民間・オルタナティブデータによる調査
の代替、という三段階で整理した。これらの効果を実現するには、以下の課題を乗り越える必
要がある。重要なのは、各段階にそれぞれ対応する壁が存在し、電子化から削減、代替へと進
んで効果が大きくなるほど、制度・技術・組織面で乗り越えるべき壁も高くなることである
（図表1）。

図表 1 「効果」の段階と対応する主な「課題」

（出所）大和総研作成

具体的には、以下のような壁が存在する。 
制度・法：行政記録情報や企業保有データを統計に利用する際には、統計法・個別法令上の
利用目的や提供範囲、守秘義務、アクセス手続を整理する必要がある。マイナンバー・法人番
号についても、それぞれの制度上認められた利用範囲を踏まえた手当てが要る。 
標準化・相互運用性：調査ごとに異なる定義・コードの不統一、企業内データと統計上の定
義との粒度ギャップが、データ連携の障害となる。 
府省の縦割り・共通基盤：調査が府省ごとに個別設計され、重複が生じやすい。事業所母集
団データベースは、統計調査結果や行政記録情報を統合し、年次フレームを各府省に提供する
共通基盤として既に運用されている。しかし、重複報告の解消に向けては、収録情報の拡充、
更新頻度の向上、各府省による一層の利活用が課題となる。 
代表性・継続性：民間・オルタナティブデータは統計作成を目的とせずに収集され、サンプ
ルの偏りが生じうる。民間依存ゆえ公表が突然停止されるリスクもある。さらに、政府側も実
証事業として民間・オルタナティブデータを活用した 公的統計を公表している場合、予算等の
関係で公表が停止することもある。例えば、経済産業省のBigData-STATS は2022 年4 月を最後
に更新されておらず、米国商務省経済分析局（BEA）のNear Real-Time Spendingも予算制約に
より 2024 年5 月7 日を最後に更新を終了した。 
品質を担保する仕組み：公的統計には、統計法に加え、公的統計の品質保証に関する政府横

1 溝端幹雄［2026］「なぜ企業は同じ情報を何度も提出しなければならないのか：公的統計のデジタル化を問
う①－負担軽減の『効果』」、大和総研レポート（2026 年10 月16 日） 
「効果」の段階 主な「課題」 手当ての方向
① 電子化 利用率の低迷、システム・様式の不統一 共通認証の徹底、様式標準化、レスポンシブ対応
② 削減 統計法・個別法令上の利用条件、府省間の縦割り、共通基盤の連携不足 利用制度の整備、ビジネスレジスターの高度化
③ 代替 民間・オルタナティブデータの代表性・継続性、共通的な品質評価手順の未整備 公的統計との補完設計、品質ルール・PETs

### Page 3

1

3 / 9

断的な指針や統計委員会による審議・評価の枠組みがある。一方、民間データを公的統計に利
用する場合には、提供主体、対象範囲、生成過程、継続性などがデータごとに異なるため、代
表性や安定性を評価する共通の基準、検証手順、メタデータの整備を一層進める必要がある。 
コストとインセンティブ：企業側のシステム対応・データ整備にはコストがかかる。負担を
求める一方でメリットの還元がなければ、協力は続かない。 
機密・セキュリティ・信頼：企業機密や情報漏洩への懸念に加え、過去の基幹統計調査をめ
ぐる信頼の毀損もあり、安全性と信頼の確保が前提となる。 
人材・予算：統計人材の確保やデジタル投資の財源が不足しがちで、予算制約が根底にある 。

ガバナンス改革：誰が旗を振るか 
上記の課題の多くは、個別の対策だけでは解決が難しく、統計を作る体制そのものに関わる。
前編でも指摘したように、日本は各府省が所管分野の統計を作成する分散型統計機構を採用し
ている。所管分野の専門性を活かせる長所がある一方、様式・定義の標準化や重複排除、府省
共通基盤の整備といった横断的な取組みでは 課題が残りやすい。そのため、総務省による審
査・調整や統計委員会による審議・勧告を、各府省の実施に確実につなげる仕組みが重要とな
る。once-only 原則や共通基盤の整備は、まさにこの横断領域にあたる。 
2018 年の統計法改正（統計法及び独立行政法人統計センター法の一部を改正する法律、平成
30 年法律第 34 号）では、統計委員会に、「統計及び統計制度の発達（筆者注：社会経済の変化
や利用者ニーズに応じて、統計と統計制度を新たに整備・充実・高度化していくこと）及び改
善」に関する基本的事項の調査審議や、基本計画の実施状況に関する勧告などの権限が追加さ
れ、府省横断の調整機能が強化された。しかし、共通基盤の整備や重複報告の解消を実行に移
す上では、なお調整力や実施体制に課題が残る。諸外国の統計機構は大きく「集権型 」「分散
型」に分けることができるが（図表 2）、両者は明確な形で分かれているわけではなく、さらに
「分散型」にも中央機関による基準設定や府省横断調整が強い国（英国や米国） 、各作成主体の
自律性が相対的に強い国（日本）がある。このため、制度類型だけで実行力の差を説明するこ
とは難しい。重要なのは、各国の制度配置そのものよりも、標準化、データアクセス、予算・
人材配分を横断的に調整し、実施を担保する権限と仕組みである。 
現行の第 IV 期の公的統計基本計画（以下、基本計画）の最終年度である 2027 年度を見据え、
次期の第 V 期基本計画では、統計委員会の権限・体制の強化や、府省横断でデータ整備を統括
する機能（諸外国のChief Statistician的な役割）の在り方を、正面から論点に据えるべきで
ある。技術や制度の議論に先立って「誰が旗を振るのか」を定めることが、実装の前提となる。

4 / 9

（注）各国の統計機構は明確な形で分散型・集権型に分かれているわけではない。 さらに、分散型にも中央機
関による基準設定や府省横断調整が強い国 （英国や米国） 、各作成主体の自律性が相対的に強い国（日本）が
ある。 
（出所）大和総研作成

諸外国の先行事例に学ぶ 
日本が向かうべき方向は、諸外国の取組みに具体的な手がかりがある。共通するのは、行政
記録情報や民間データの利用を「その都度の交渉」ではなく「制度」として位置づけ、企業に
同じ情報を二度書かせない仕組み（once-only 原則）を整えている点である（図表3） 。

図表 3 報告者負担軽減に関する諸外国の先行事例

（出所）各国統計機関・欧州委員会資料より大和総研作成

行政記録情報の統計利用（英国） ：英国国家統計局（ONS）は、2017 年 12 月公表の四半期国
民経済計算から、VAT 申告に基づく売上高データを GDP の生産側推計の一部に取り入れた。こ
れは月次企業調査を全面的に代替するものではなく、主に中小企業のデータを補完し、調査デ
ータと組み合わせて利用する仕組みである。さらに、景気変化を早期に捉える Faster 
indicators の取組みでは、VAT 申告に基づく月次指標を研究成果として公表した。ただし、こ
の指標は公式の GDP 統計を代替・予測するものではない。企業に追加の調査票を課さず、既存
の申告データを統計作成に活用する事例として位置づけられる。 
標準ビジネスレポーティング（オランダ・豪州） ：報告項目の定義とデータ様式を標準化し、
企業の会計・業務システムに記録された情報を、複数の行政報告に再利用しやすくする標準ビ
ジネスレポーティング（Standard Business Reporting： SBR）を進めている。会計システムか
ら標準様式で提出できれば、報告ごとの転記や再加工の負担を減らせる。 
論点
分散型
（日本、米国、英国 等）
〔中央調整機能の強さには国ごとに差〕
集権型
（カナダ、豪州、オランダ 等）
統計の作成主体 各府省が所管分野を個別に作成 中央統計局が主要統計を中心的に作成
標準化・重複排除 府省間調整を要し、中央調整機能が弱い場合には推進が難しい 中央が主導しやすい
長所／短所 専門性が高い／横断連携が弱い 連携・効率が高い／専門の分散に留意
国・地域 取組 報告者負担の観点での含意
英国 VAT申告データをGDP推計の一部に導入（2017年～）。2019年から研究系列の月次Faster indicatorsも公表 行政記録利用で「そもそも聞かない」
オランダ・豪州 標準ビジネスレポーティング（SBR） 一度の提出を複数報告で再利用
北欧（デンマーク等） 登録簿（レジスター）で国勢調査を代替 調査票なしで抜本的に軽減
エストニア・EU 行政記録の利用根拠を整備。EUでは対象行政手続にonce-only原則を導入 対象手続で既提出情報の再提出を抑制

### Page 5

1

5 / 9

レジスター型統計（北欧） ：行政記録を統合した登録簿（レジスター）を統計の基盤に据え、
デンマークなどは国勢調査を調査票なしにレジスターで代替している。報告者負担の抜本的な
軽減と統計作成の効率化を両立させた到達点である。 
制度的な取得根拠（エストニア・ EU）：エストニアでは、公式統計法に基づいて統計作成者
が行政記録情報等を利用する仕組みが設けられている。また、行政全体では、法制度、電子ID、
官民のデータベースを安全に接続する X-Road®2等を基盤として、同じ情報を複数の行政機関に
繰り返し提出させない once-only 原則が実装されている。EU でも、単一デジタルゲートウェイ
（Single Digital Gateway：SDG ）3規則などを通じて、対象となる行政手続におけるonce-only
原則の実装が進められている。 
これらに照らすと、日本は行政記録情報の統計利用や府省共通基盤の整備がなお途上にある。
各国が「制度」で担保していることを、日本も次期第 V 期基本計画で制度に落とし込めるかが
問われる。

機密・信頼を「技術」で担保する：PETs 
企業機密や情報漏洩への懸念は、データ提供・連携をためらわせる大きな壁 の一つである。
とりわけ行政記録や民間データを統計に利活用しようとすれば、 「自社の詳細な情報が、目的
外に使われたり漏れたりしないか」という懸念に必ず突き当たる。この懸念は、守秘義務や罰
則といった制度だけでなく、最近では「技術」によっても軽減できるようになってきた。 
その中核が、プライバシー強化技術（Privacy-Enhancing Technologies：PETs）である。代
表例として、複数の当事者が生データを互いに共有せずに計算する「秘密計算（安全な多者間
計算等） 」 、暗号化したまま計算する「準同型暗号」 、集計結果等から個人・個票に関する情報
が推測されるリスクを数理的に制御する「差分プライバシー」 、実データの統計的性質を一定
程度再現した擬似データを用いる「合成データ」がある（ 図表 4） 。国連公的統計ビッグデー
タ・データサイエンス専門家委員会（UN Committee of Experts on Big Data and Data Science 
for Official Statistics：UN- CEBD）は、2023 年に公的統計向けの PETs ガイドを公表してお
り 4、各国統計機関等による実証や実装事例も蓄積されている。これらは、 「データを集めて一
か所に置く」従来の方式に加え、データを移動・開示せずに必要な計算結果を得る選択肢を広
げる。 
日本でも、行政記録情報や民間データを統計に利活用する際に、 「制度（守秘・罰則） 」と
「技術（PETs） 」を両輪として安全性と信頼を確保することが、企業の協力を得る前提となる。

2 e-Estonia, “X-Road”. 
3 EU 域内で国境を越えて生活・事業を行う市民や企業に対して、各国の行政情報・オンライン行政手続・支援
サービスへの共通の入口を提供する制度。 
4 United Nations [2023], United Nations Guide on Privacy-Enhancing Technologies for Official Statistics, United 
Nations Committee of Experts on Big Data and Data Science for Official Statistics, New York.

技術で守れる範囲を広げることは、そのまま「安心して出せる」範囲を広げることにつながる。

図表 4 主なプライバシー強化技術（PETs）の類型

（出所）United Nations[2023], United Nations Guide on Privacy-Enhancing Technologies for Official Statistics よ
り大和総研作成

官民データ供給の制度・経済設計 
民間データの代表性・継続性のリスクは、 「どのように安定して供給を受けるか」の制度・
経済設計によって緩和できる。民間データは統計作成を目的に集められておらず、提供主体の
事業判断によって公表・提供が止まりうる。さらに、政府が実証事業として民間・オルタナテ
ィブデータを活用した公的統計を公表している場合、先述した日本や米国の例のように、政府
の予算制約によってその実証事業が中止されることもある。民間データが安定 的に供給される
には、次の三つの手段を組み合わせる必要がある。 
・法的な提供根拠 ― 統計上の必要性、公益性、代替手段の有無、企業負担、営業秘密の保
護等を踏まえ、対象データと利用条件を限定した提供根拠を整備する。 
・対価・インセンティブ ― 提供に見合う便益や費用補填を用意し、継続的な協力を促す。 
・標準的な提供手続・品質基準 ― データの様式・品質を揃え、継続的な受領と一定の品質
担保を可能にする。 
海外では、民間データへのアクセスや共有を支える枠組みの整備が進む。欧州データ法
（Data Act ）は、コネクテッド製品 5や関連サービスから生じるデータへのアクセスや利用に関
する横断的なルールを定めるとともに、例外的な必要性がある場合に公的機関が民間保有デー
タを求める仕組みを設けた6。また、欧州共通データスペース（Common European Data Spaces）
は、分野別のデータ共有基盤や相互運用性の整備を進めている 7。もっとも、これらは公的統
計への民間データの恒常的な供給を一般的に義務づける制度ではない。日本でも、統計目的で
民間データを継続利用する場合の根拠、手続、費用負担、品質管理を個別に制度設計する必要
がある（図表5）。

5 自動車の走行・故障データ、機械の稼働・保守データ、スマート家電の利用状況データなど、IoT 製品。 
6 European Commission, “Data Act explained”。根拠条文は規則（EU）2023/2854 の第2 章・第5 章（特に
第 14～15 条）（2025 年 12 月15 日最終更新）。 
7 European Commission, “Common European data spaces”。 
技術 概要 主な用途
秘密計算（安全な多者間計算等） 生データを共有せず統計値のみ算出 府省・官民をまたぐ集計
準同型暗号 暗号化したまま計算 機密データの安全な処理
差分プライバシー 集計結果等から個人・個票に関する情報が推測されるリスクを数学的に制御 統計の安全な公表
合成データ 実データの統計的性質を一定程度再現した擬似データを生成 分析・研究、システム開発、安全性を評価した上での外部提供

第 V期公的統計基本計画への提言 
公的統計の整備に関する基本計画は、統計法に基づきおおむね 5 年ごとに策定される。現行
の第 IV 期基本計画は 2023 年 3 月に閣議決定され、2023 年度～2027 年度を対象とする。第 IV
期基本計画の最終年度である2027 年度を見据え、次期基本計画では企業の報告者負担の軽減を
制度に組み込むことが重要となる。そしてこれは企業の生産性の改善にもつながる。前編・後
編の議論を踏まえ、提言の柱として以下を示したい（図表6） 。

図表 6 第 V 期公的統計基本計画に向けた主な論点

（出所）大和総研作成

柱1 once-only原則の明文化：行政が既に保有・取得済みの情報は、原則として企業に再度
提出を求めない。重複報告の解消を制度の基本方針に据える。 
柱 2 行政記録・民間データ利活用の制度整備： 統計目的での安全な利活用が進むよう、統
計法と個別の行政記録を所管する法令との関係を整理し、利用目的、提供範囲、アクセス権限、
保存期間、監査方法を明確にする。既存の守秘義務や目的外利用制限の水準を後退させるので
はなく、必要な保護措置を維持した上で、PETs を含む技術的・組織的安全管理措置を制度に位
置づける。 
柱 3 府省共通の報告基盤：事業所母集団データベース（日本のビジネスレジスター）の母
集団情報・重複是正機能を高度化するとともに、標準様式、共通認証、政府統計オンライン調
査総合窓口等を連携させ、 「一度出せば足りる」報告環境を府省横断で整備する。 
手段 内容・国内外の例
法的根拠（義務化） 行政記録等を統計目的で利用する根拠を法令に規定。民間保有データについては対象・利用条件を限定した制度設計が必要
データアクセスルールと共有・相互運
用基盤の整備
欧州データ法によるデータアクセスルール（公的機関の取得は例外的必要性がある場合等に限
定）、欧州共通データスペースによる相互運用・共有基盤
対価・品質基準 提供の対価・費用補填、標準様式・品質基準による継続的受領
柱 主な課題 第V期に向けた提言の方向性
柱1　once-only原則の明文化 重複報告、府省ごとの様式・定義の不統一、共通認証の不足 既提出情報の再提出を原則不要とし、標準様式・共通認証で重複報告を解消
柱2　行政記録・民間データ利活
用の制度整備
法令上の利用条件、アクセス手続、代表性・継続性・品
質、機密保護
利用目的・提供範囲・権限を明確化し、PETs、安全管
理、公的統計との補完設計、品質・供給基準を整備
柱3　府省共通の報告基盤 事業所母集団データベースの収録情報・更新頻度、府省横断利用、認証・報告システムの連携不足 事業所母集団データベース（日本のビジネスレジスター）の高度化、法人番号、GビズID等と連携
柱4　企業へのメリット還元 企業のシステム対応コスト、提供メリットの乏しさ、協力継続の難しさ 速報・詳細統計、安全な二次利用、フィードバック、費用補填で企業に還元
柱5　ガバナンス・人材・予算 府省横断の調整力・実施体制、統計人材、デジタル投資予算の不足 統計委員会の機能を強化し、官民連携・品質保証、人材育成、継続予算を確保

### Page 8

1

8 / 9

柱 4 企業へのメリット還元：提出データに見合う形で、統計の速報化・詳細化やミクロデ
ータの安全な二次的利用・フィードバックを企業に提供する互恵の枠組みを作る。 
柱 5 ガバナンス・人材・予算：統計委員会の機能強化、官民連携のルール作りと品質保証、
統計人材の育成、継続的な予算確保を、計画に位置づける。

実装の設計：既存インフラ・費用便益・工程表 
提言を「絵に描いた餅」にしないために、実装の道筋を三つの観点から具体化しておきたい。 
既存インフラで実装する：ゼロから作るのではなく、既存の仕組みを役割に応じて接続する。
法人番号を企業の名寄せの鍵に、 「G ビズ ID（すべての事業者を対象とする共通認証システム） 」
を行政サービスの共通認証に、「事業所母集団データベース」 を経済統計の母集団情報の基盤
に据える。「G ビズインフォ」8は、法人番号を基礎に各府省が保有する法人情報を提供する公
開情報基盤として活用しうるが、企業から統計情報を受け付ける共通窓口ではない。各基盤の
役割を明確にした上で、政府統計オンライン調査総合窓口を含む報告システムとの連携を進め
ることが、 「一度出せば足りる」基盤の現実的な構築につながる。 
費用便益を明示する：コストは主に政府のシステム投資と企業の初期対応であり、便益は負
担軽減・回収率向上による精度改善・行政効率化である。ここで重要なのは、コストを負う主
体（企業・府省）と便益を得る主体（統計利用者・社会全体）が異なる「帰属のズレ」がある
ことだ。だからこそ、メリット還元と、投資を支える財源の設計が要となる。 
工程表を伴わせる：短期（電子化・共通認証の徹底、負担計測のKPI 化）→中期（once-only
原則の制度化、事業所母集団データベースの高度化）→長期（民間データ・PETs の本格活用）
と段階を分け、2028 年度からの開始が見込まれる第V 期基本計画を起点に着実に進める（図表
7） 。一度にすべてを求めるのではなく、効果の出やすい短期施策から着手し、成功体験を積み
上げることが現実的だ。

図表 7 報告者負担軽減に向けた実装ロードマップ

（出所）大和総研作成

8 「G ビズインフォ」は、経済産業省が提供する政府保有法人情報の公開サイトである。国税庁「法人番号公
表サイト」で公表されている法人基本 3 情報（法人番号、法人名、本店所在地）に、各府省が保有する財務情
報、決算情報、特許情報、届出・認定情報、補助金情報、調達情報、表彰情報、職場情報等を紐づけ、二次利
用可能なオープンデータとして提供している（https://info.gbiz.go.jp/）。 
時期 主な取組
短期 オンライン回答の拡大、GビズID等を活用した共通認証との連携検討、報告者負担の計測・KPI化、様式・定義の標準化
中期 once-only原則の基本方針への明記、事業所母集団データベースと各府省の報告基盤との連携強化、行政記録の統計利用の拡大
長期 民間データの継続利用を支える制度の整備とPETsの本格活用、行政記録等で代替可能な分野からのレジスター型統計への段階的移行、企業への便益還元の定着

段階的移行の留意点 ― 結びに代えて 
以上、今回の前編・後編レポートの内容を図表8 にまとめた。 
最後に、移行にあたっての留意点を述べておきたい。デジタル化を進める際には、デジタル
デバイドへの配慮が欠かせない。小規模事業所や高齢の記入者、社内の稟議に紙の様式を要す
る企業も一定数残る。オンライン・once-only 原則を推進しつつ、移行期間中は調査の特性や
回答者の利用環境に応じて紙などの代替的な回答手段を確保し、コールセンター等による支援
を続けることが、回収率の維持につながる。一律かつ急速な移行は、負担軽減どころか回収率
を押し下げる可能性がある。 
目指すべきは、効果の出やすい施策から着実に積み上げる、段階的で現実的な移行である。
これが、報告者負担の軽減と統計精度の確保を両立させる道である。 
統計の利用面では、デジタル化やグローバル化などの経済構造の変化をより適切に捉える新
たな国際基準である 2025SNA が、2025 年 3 月の第 56 回国連統計委員会で国民経済計算の国際
統計基準として採択され、日本でも導入に向けた検討が進められている。統計の作成基盤と測
定基準の双方でデジタル化への対応が進む中、行政記録や民間データを安全かつ継続的に利用
できる体制は、企業に「より少ない負担で、より役立つ統計」を実現する基盤となる。次期基
本計画は、報告者負担の軽減を持続可能性確保の中核に据える機会とすべきである。企業・政
府・統計利用者が、負担と便益を分かち合いながら制度を支える。その設計こそが、公的統計
の持続可能性を左右する。

図表 8 今回のレポート（前編・後編）のまとめ

（出所）大和総研作成 
以上 
公的統計のデジタル化を問う：「一度出せば足りる」統計への転換
企業の報告者負担を抜本的に軽減し、次期（第V期）基本計画に向けた実装を急げ
現状と課題：制度の持続可能性を脅かす「重い報告者負担」
構造的問題：分散型統計制度による類似項目の「重複報告」が常態化。
負担は専門担当を置きにくい中小企業ほど固定費的に重い（逆進性）。
危機の連鎖：負担増による「回収率の低下」が進行。企業系調査は
81.8%→76.2%へ、世帯系は95.7%→62.6%へ急落し、推計精度と
制度基盤が揺らいでいる。
解決の方向性：負担軽減を実現する「デジタル化の3段階」
STEP 1: 電子化
（入力チャネルの移行）
STEP 2: 削減
（そもそも聞かない）
STEP 3: 代替
（調査自体の代替）
オンライン調査の推進。
ただし「同じ項目を何度
も入力させる」構造的負
担は残る。
行政が一度得た情報は求
めない「once-only原
則」の徹底。行政記録や
企業システムからの標準
データ連携。
POS・スキャナー等
の民間・オルタナ
ティブデータを活用
した調査の代替・補
完。
【第4の層：生成AI】
下書き自動生成や自動符号づけ等で各段階を横断的に実装支援
実装の壁を越える：機密性の確保とガバナンス
「技術」による信頼確保：企業最大の懸念（情報漏洩）は制度だけ
でなく、秘密計算や合成データ等のPETs（プライバシー強化技術）
導入で技術的に担保可能。
司令塔の必要性：府省の縦割りを排し、標準化やデータ連携を横断
推進するガバナンス改革（統計委員会の権限強化等）が必須。
提言：第V期 公的統計基本計画に向けた「5つの柱」
1. once-only原則の明文化：重複報告の削減を制度の基本方針に据
え、負担削減をPDCA化。
2. データ利活用の制度整備：統計法と個別法令を整理し、民間デー
タ・行政記録の安定的な供給ルールを構築。
3. 府省共通の報告基盤：事業所母集団データベース、法人番号、Gビ
ズID等を連携させ、「一度出せば足りる」環境を政府横断で構築。
4. 企業へのメリット還元：データの速報化や詳細なベンチマーク提供
により、企業を統計の「出す側」から「使う側」へと位置づけ直す。
5. ガバナンス・人材・予算：統計委員会の権限強化と、着実な工程
表に基づく投資財源の確保。

《東協尚未擺脫整理，美印關係改善訊號浮現》
9月MSCI東協指數下跌 2.6%，雖然全球公債殖利率
與油價走升帶來評價面壓力 ，但從股價跌幅及外資
賣超規模來看 ，本波修正仍偏向短線漲多後的技術
性調節。修正後，東協股市本益比已由高於近兩年
平均2倍標準差回落至約 1倍標準差 ，RSI亦降至40
以下，顯示市場過熱情況明顯改善 。然而在聯準會
升息及殖利率維持高檔的背景下 ，若基本面未能提
供更多上修空間 ，預期股市短期仍將維持震盪整理
格局。印度方面，9月回檔已使股價回測 3月低點，
本益比降至 19.9倍，RSI跌破30，顯示短線賣壓已

有過度反應。此外，莫迪於9月金磚峰會公開反對去
美元化，近期川普亦表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，反映
美印經貿關係出現改善訊號 。後續可持續關注雙邊
合作進展及其對印度中長期投資影響。

響，並加大非科技類股及非科技市場的修正幅度 。

的獲利了結與資產重新配置 ，而非反映市場對區域
基本面的明顯轉弱或出現恐慌性撤資。

能維持較市場預期更久 ，意味短期經濟成長動能將
更仰賴外需及製造業活動的支撐。

《川普表態支持印歐經濟走廊 ，重構印度樞紐角色 》
川普近期表態支持印歐經濟走廊 (IMEC)，使這項拜
登任內支持，但因加薩戰爭與區域衝突而停滯近3年
的計畫再度受到市場關注 。隨著中東地緣風險升溫 ，
紅海及荷姆茲海峽等關鍵航道不確定性提高 ，各國
對替代性能源與運輸路徑的需求同步增加 。從規劃
路線來看 ，印歐經濟走廊以印度為起點 ，串連海灣
產油國與歐洲市場 ，若計畫順利推進 ，除有助印度
取得更穩定的中東能源供給 、降低對俄羅斯石油的
依賴外 ，也將進一步鞏固其作為全球製造與供應鏈
樞紐的地位 。對印股而言 ，IMEC有望同時強化能源
安全與供應鏈地位 ，鞏固印度在全球製造的地位 ，
並為基建、工業及製造類股帶來中長期題材支撐。

重點摘要
• 川普表態支持擱置近 3年的印
歐經濟走廊重啟 ，在中東地
緣風險升溫下 ，有助印度強
化能源安全 、降低對俄油依
賴，並鞏固全球製造與供應
鏈樞紐地位。

主題報告概述 : 本篇報告將分析 2026年美元現況 ，最終形成
2027年美元看法。以下為本篇報告內容概要:
(1) 匯市現況：美國伊朗戰爭導致通膨疑慮再起，美元走強。
(2) 政治議題：2026年共和黨失去絕對執政對美元的影響。
(3) 期中選後風險因子：財政、貿易戰、美伊戰爭。
(4) 美元基本面：景氣與通膨循環將維持在金髮女孩經濟環境。
(5) 美元技術面：技術面跌破年線，籌碼面偏多但8月轉弱。
(6) 非美匯市: 歐洲政治面利空及日本阻貶為美元提供底部支撐
匯市結論: 美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭 、

油價上漲及通膨預期升溫支撐 ，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治
與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。期中選舉後 ，
共和黨失去完全執政優勢 ，國會分裂將限制大規模財政擴張 ，
有助抑制通膨與赤字惡化 ，但也降低進一步刺激經濟成長的動
能。展望2027年，美國仍面臨債務上限 、財政協商、貿易政策、
美伊衝突及烏俄戰爭等風險 ，其中地緣政治與油價走勢仍是左
右美元的重要變數 。2027年美元並非進入新一輪強勢多頭週期 ，
而是在高利率支撐下維持區間整理 。2027年美國經濟基準情境
穩健，景氣溫和成長 ，通膨從高檔放緩 。若無重大地緣政治升
級事件，美元指數預期維持97–105區間震盪，並呈現「上有壓
力、下有支撐 」的盤整格局 。當地緣政治升溫時美元偏強 ，當
通膨回落與利率下降時美元偏弱。
